⏺ I've reviewed the repo. Here's what I found and what I plan to do. One
  decision needs your call before I start.

  Cleanup review (read-only so far)
  - Every tracked file is in use. Each doc, script, compose file and test
    package is referenced from somewhere: README, DECISIONS, the Makefile, CI or
    compose. I found nothing that is clearly safe to delete.
  - The local clutter (.DS_Store, .superpowers/*.log, e2e/shots*) is already
    gitignored, so it won't reach the branch. I'll leave it alone.
  - One duplicated responsibility: writeJSON, writeError and serverError are
    defined separately in auth, campaign and provider. Each copy is 3 lines and
    private to its package. Merging them would mean a new shared package for
    about 10 lines, which is the kind of refactoring you asked me to avoid. I'll
    mention it in the PR and won't change it.
  - So the cleanup step will probably only touch the README. If you know of
    something specific you want removed, tell me.

  Plan (one branch docs/openapi-readme, draft PR, no merge)
  1. OpenAPI 3.1 spec as a hand-written internal/apidocs/openapi.json, embedded
     into the binary with go:embed.
     - It covers all 23 /api endpoints: login/logout/me, catalog, dashboard, the
       campaign routes, the SSE events stream and recipients.
     - It documents the scc_session cookie auth, request and response schemas,
       and the error shape {error, code?, field_errors?, unsupported?}.
     - It lists the status codes the handlers actually return: 400, 401, 403,
       404, 409, 422, 429, 502, 504 and 500.
     - It goes in internal/ and not docs/ because .dockerignore excludes docs/,
       so the image build couldn't embed it from there. I chose JSON so tests
       can parse it with the standard library and no YAML dependency.
  2. GET /docs (the Swagger UI page) and GET /docs/openapi.json, served by the
     api process only.
     - Both are public: the spec holds no secrets, and the API itself still
       requires login.
     - /docs gets its own CSP. The app's strict CSP stays unchanged.
     - No API behavior changes.
  3. TDD test: every operation in the spec must resolve to a real route on the
     actual muxes, and the spec's route count must match. That keeps the spec
     from drifting out of date. A second test covers /docs: it serves the page,
     the spec and the CSP.
  4. Shorter README: keep setup, demo login, architecture, features, testing/CI,
     limitations and links to CASE, DECISIONS, docs, QA evidence and /docs.
     Remove the long explanations that repeat DECISIONS.md.
  5. A DECISIONS.md entry for the API docs, go test for the touched packages,
     make lint, then push, open a draft PR and wait for CI.

  DECISIONS.md, ai-sessions/, docs/qa/ and CASE.md stay as they are; the only
  change to DECISIONS.md is the new entry.

         -eturns 422 (with `field_errors` for rules); an "unsupported" br

         -rns 502/504 and nothing is saved; a 502 carries `code` `ai_inva
         -lid` (the answer failed validation twice) or `ai_unavailable` (
         -the provider failed, e.g. a rejected API key or an outage), so 
         -the UI tells them apart. Content generation reports the same fa
         -ilures as an `error` event in its stream, with the same `code`.

       66 +## Features                                                   
       67  
      101 -The `mock-provider` service plays the SMS and email provider; 
          -nothing is really sent. `POST /sms/send` `{"to", "body"}` and 
          -`POST /email/send` `{"to", "subject", "body"}` require an `Ide
          -mpotency-Key` header (the message ID). Per channel it adds a d
          -elay and fails a share of requests, with the defaults from D20
          -: 20–200 ms delay, 5% 5xx, 3% 429 with `Retry-After: 1`, 1% re
          -quests that hang past the worker's 5 s timeout and are then ac
          -cepted (a lost response). Change them with the `SMS_*` / `EMAI
          -L_*` settings in `.env.example`; `PROVIDER_SEED` makes runs re
          -peatable.                                                     
       68 +- **Brief → audience:** the AI maps the brief to rules from a 
          +fixed catalog (city, cart and order history, spend, categories
          +, sign-up date; D14, D70), or says it can't. Rules are validat
          +ed and shown as editable chips with live counts per channel; t
          +hey can also be built by hand.                                
       69 +- **Content:** the AI streams SMS and email text per language 
          +(Turkish, English) into editors; text is personalised with `{{
          +first_name}}`, `{{last_name}}`, `{{city}}` and length-checked 
          +after personalisation (SMS 160, subject 80, email body 2,000).
          + Each language is reviewed and approved on its own (D64–D67). 
          +Edits save automatically with optimistic locking.             
       70 +- **Approval:** nothing is sent without an explicit *Send now*
          + or *Schedule* (1 minute to 30 days ahead) and a second confir
          +mation; estimates are labelled as estimates (D30).            
       71 +- **Sending:** the worker builds the audience at send time, re
          +-checks consent and cancellation before each request, sends wi
          +th an idempotency key, retries 429/5xx/timeouts with backoff, 
          +and stays within 100 requests per second per channel (D19, D69
          +). Over-limit messages are skipped and can be resent (D31).   
       72 +- **Monitoring:** a live status stream per campaign (sent, unc
          +ertain, failed, skipped, cancelled, retrying, pending, with re
          +asons), recipient details with masked contacts (D46, D47), can
          +cel and resend.                                               
       73 +- **A/B test (optional):** version B per language, a reproduci
          +ble 50/50 split, delivery results per version; no winner is na
          +med, since no outcome data exists (D68).                      
       74 +- **Dashboard and list:** status counts, server-side search, f
          +ilters and paging (D54). English and Turkish UI (D44).        
       75  
      103 -The key is stored before anything is simulated. A repeated key
          - gets the stored result for a finished request, or a retryable
          - 503 with `Retry-After` while it is still in progress; a 5xx o
          -r 429 releases the key so a retry is processed. Keys live in t
          -he `provider` schema, which the provider reaches through its o
          -wn database role (`scc_provider`, password `PROVIDER_DB_PASSWO
          -RD`) that cannot read the app's tables (D6).

      105 -## Sending                                                    
       78 +One Go module (`cmd/scc`) built into one image and run as Comp
          +ose services (D20): `postgres`, `migrate` (one-shot migrations
          + and seed), `api`, `worker` and `mock-provider`. The Vue 3 + T
          +ypeScript frontend (`web/`) is built into the same image and s
          +erved by the api.                                             
       79  
      107 -The `worker` service starts approved campaigns when their send
          - time comes, evaluating the audience at that moment (D30), and
          - sends each message through the provider with its message ID a
          -s `Idempotency-Key`. Before every request it re-checks that th
          -e campaign is still sending and the recipient still allows the
          - channel, and it personalizes and length-checks the text: over
          --limit messages are skipped (`character_limit_exceeded`) and c
          -an be resent later (D31). Each message uses the text of the la
          -nguage fixed for it when the audience was built (D64). 200 → `
          -sent`; 429, 5xx, timeouts and network errors are retried with 
          -exponential backoff and jitter (honouring `Retry-After`) up to
          - `MAX_ATTEMPTS` (5); other 4xx → `failed`. Every attempt is re
          -corded in `delivery_attempts`.                                
       80 +| Package | Responsibility |                                  
       81 +|---|---|                                                     
       82 +| `internal/auth` | Login, logout, session cookie check, sign-
          +in throttling, same-origin check on writes |                  
       83 +| `internal/campaign` | The campaign API: drafts, content, app
          +roval, estimates, live status, recipients, resend |           
       84 +| `internal/audience` | Rule catalog and validation; rules bec
          +ome parameterized SQL (values only ever reach Postgres as argu
          +ments) |                                                      
       85 +| `internal/content` | Message templates, length limits, over-
          +limit estimates, language catalog |

          +validated against a schema, never trusted |

          +ses, D5, D18), rate limits, dispatch; one sender via an adviso
          +ry lock |

       92  
      109 -A request without a clear answer (a timeout or a network error
          -) may still have been delivered, so the message is marked `del
          -ivery_uncertain`; the retry uses the same key and gets the pro
          -vider's stored result instead of a second send. The same holds
          - when a worker died mid-attempt (its expired lease is handed b
          -ack by the running worker within a second of expiring). If the
          - campaign is cancelled or the recipient opts out before that r
          -etry, the message is recorded as cancelled or skipped with a n
          -ote that delivery is uncertain, and a later resend leaves them
          - out (D31). Stopping the worker interrupts in-flight requests:
          - they are recorded as `interrupted`, do not count toward `MAX_
          -ATTEMPTS`, and the message is retried at once; messages still 
          -waiting for a rate-limit slot go straight back to the queue.  
       93 +The provider's failure rates and delays are set with the `SMS_
          +*` / `EMAIL_*` settings in `.env.example` (defaults: 20–200 ms
          +, 5% 5xx, 3% 429, 1% lost responses).                         
       94  
      111 -Per channel at most 100 provider requests leave in any second,
          - retries included: a rate limiter spaces them 10 ms apart and 
          -a hard cap counts each departure after the last database check
          -, right before the request leaves (D19, D69). The tests check 
          -both the recorded departures and what the provider received. N
          -etwork delays of more than 50 ms between the worker and the pr
          -ovider could in theory still bunch requests at the provider.  
       95 +## Testing                                                    
       96  
      113 -The limit lives in the worker process, so only one worker send
          -s: it holds a Postgres advisory lock (D69). A second worker (`
          -docker compose up --scale worker=2`) logs that it is waiting a
          -nd takes over when the first stops. A worker that takes the lo
          -ck waits about a second before its first send, so its first se
          -cond and the previous worker's last one can't add up to more t
          -han 100 requests. If the lock's connection fails, the worker s
          -tops sending within about 1–3 s and takes the lock again befor
          -e it continues; if its host vanished instead, the database fre
          -es the lock in about 25 s. A message that can't be read before
          - sending (a failed query, or shutdown) goes back to the queue,
          - not flagged uncertain; if the database stays unreachable, the
          - result can't be written either, and the message is flagged un
          -certain when its lease expires, as after a crash.

          -ite with AI** (asks first) and **Make default**. After the bri
          -ef changes, every language is marked "may be outdated" until i
          -t is approved again, as it is or rewritten. Text is never clea
          -red or rewritten without the marketer asking.                 
      156 -- **Review:** who gets each language's text (in their own lang
          -uage, or as the default), each language's state, a preview per
          - language and the over-limit estimate per language. Approval n
          -eeds every language approved.                                 
      157 -- **Sending:** each recipient's language is fixed when the aud
          -ience is built (`messages.language`). Retries keep it; a resen
          -d fixes its own recipients' languages from its own languages. 
      158 -- **Results:** the recipient details show each message's langu
          -age, with a filter.                                           
      159 -- **Campaigns from before T20:** their content shows as "Langu
          -age not recorded", is sent to everyone as before, and can be g
          -iven a language.                                              
      160 -                                                              
      161 -**A/B test** (T21, D68). Optional, off by default; not on rese
          -nds.                                                          
      162 -                                                              
      163 -- **Content step:** *Compare two versions (A/B test)* adds an 
          -**A | B** switch inside each language tab. Version B is writte
          -n by hand, started with **Copy version A**, or written with **
          -Write version B with AI** (only for languages without one, nev
          -er touching A). Approving a language approves both versions; e
          -diting either one needs review again.                         
      164 -- **Split:** when the audience is built, each language's uniqu
          -e recipients are ordered by `md5(campaign:user)` and alternate
          -d A, B, so the split is 50/50 within one recipient per languag
          -e, reproducible, and the same on every channel. The version is
          - stored on each message (`messages.variant`); retries reuse it
          -.                                                             
      165 -- **Text after approval:** an approved campaign's content can'
          -t be edited, so every attempt, retries included, sends the app
          -roved text of the recipient's language and version.           
      166 -- **Review:** versions A and B side by side per language, each
          - with its over-limit estimate.                                
      167 -- **Results:** per channel, a table per version (recipients, s
          -ent, failed, skipped, too long, waiting or retrying), and a ve
          -rsion filter in the recipient details. The page says it has de
          -livery data only: the app records no opens, clicks or orders, 
          -so it can't say which version works better, and it names no wi
          -nner.                                                         
      168 -                                                              
      169 -The review step (T17): rules, the estimated audience, the mess
          -ages and the over-limit estimate in one place, and the send ch
          -oice. **Sending always needs an explicit decision:** *Send now
          -* or *Schedule* (a date and time in the browser's zone, sent a
          -s UTC; between 1 minute and 30 days ahead), a tick that the au
          -dience and messages were checked, and a second confirmation th
          -at repeats the estimates. A time suggested from the brief is p
          -reselected only if it is still usable, labelled as a suggestio
          -n with the brief's own words; otherwise nothing is selected. U
          -ntil everything is in place, the approve button lists what is 
          -missing.                                                      
      170 -                                                              
      171 -The dashboard and the campaign list (T18): the dashboard count
          -s campaigns by status (each card opens the list filtered by it
          -) and lists six campaigns, live and waiting ones first, each w
          -ith its next step. The campaign list filters by status and sea
          -rches the brief (ignoring case and Turkish letters); both are 
          -kept in the address, so a reload or Back keeps them. Each row 
          -shows the channels, status, creation day and send time, links 
          -a resend to its original campaign, and offers what the status 
          -allows: delete a draft or cancel an approved or sending campai
          -gn (each asks first), or open it. The server filters, searches
          - and pages the list (25 per page) and counts the statuses over
          - all campaigns (D54).                                         
      172 -                                                              
      173 -After approval, the campaign's page shows its delivery (T18), 
          -all from the live status stream (D36):                        
      174 -                                                              
      175 -- **Scheduled:** when sending starts, the audience rules and t
          -he estimate made at approval (the actual audience is calculate
          -d when sending starts, D30), the approved messages (locked), a
          -nd *Cancel campaign…*.                                        
      176 -- **Sending, completed or cancelled:** estimate vs actual audi
          -ence; totals for sent, not sent, uncertain and in progress; pe
          -r channel a progress bar and the seven groups with their reaso
          -ns (D46), retries and 429s (in the last 5 minutes and in total
          -). While sending, the counts update live with the time they we
          -re taken; *Live · pause* stops and resumes updates, and *Cance
          -l campaign…* stops what hasn't been sent. A finished campaign 
          -shows read-only results and closes the stream. A campaign canc
          -elled before sending says no messages were created.           
      177 -- A resend links to its original campaign, and the original li
          -nks to an active resend.                                      
      178 -- **Recipient details** (D47): every count on the delivery pag
          -e opens a side panel listing the recipients in that group, wit
          -h tabs for the seven groups and their counts, a channel switch
          -, a name search (ignoring case and Turkish letters), the reaso
          -ns and what they mean, and per recipient the masked contact, t
          -he reason (with the length for a character-limit skip), the at
          -tempt count and an expandable attempt history (time, HTTP stat
          -us, outcome). 50 rows per page, newest attempt first. It shows
          - a snapshot taken when it opens or a filter changes, with that
          - time. The group, channel, search and page are in the address,
          - so Back closes it, Escape returns focus to the count that ope
          -ned it, and a link opens it directly. Contacts are masked by t
          -he server; no full phone number or email reaches the browser. 
      179 -- **Resend** (D31, D48): once a campaign has finished, each ch
          -annel with recipients skipped for the character limit offers *
          -Resend to ~N…* (also on the recipient panel's Skipped tab). Th
          -e dialog shows how many were skipped, how many can be reached 
          -now (an estimate, checked again when the resend starts) and wh
          -o is never included: consent revoked, uncertain deliveries (th
          -ey may already have it) and failures. *Create the resend draft
          -* opens the follow-up's content step, where the original messa
          -ge can be shown; it is approved on the review step like any ca
          -mpaign. The original then links to the resend, the resend and 
          -the campaign list link back, and a second resend isn't offered
          - while one is active. If someone else created it meanwhile, th
          -e dialog says so and the page shows the existing one.

      202 -## UI/UX design                                               
      203 -                                                              
      204 -The reference design is `design/smart-campaign-composer-refere
          -nce.html`, a Claude Artifact (a self-contained HTML page; open
          - it in a browser). It has ten boards: the journey overview wit
          -h the status vocabulary, sign in, dashboard, campaigns, the co
          -mposer's audience, content and review steps, live delivery, re
          -cipient details, and resend.                                  
      205 -                                                              
      206 -How it was made and is used:                                  
      207 -                                                              
      208 -1. **From requirements to a journey.** The flow follows `CASE.
          -md` and the decisions: brief → editable audience rules → strea
          -med content → explicit approval → monitoring. Numbers before s
          -ending starts are labelled estimates; numbers after it are act
          -ual message outcomes (D30).                                   
      209 -2. **Prototype.** The screens were prototyped as an artifact w
          -ith mock data. Actions the API might not support were marked "
          -CONFIRM API".                                                 
      210 -3. **Review against the backend** (22 Oct 2026). Every screen 
          -was checked against the API. The gaps led to draft deletion, c
          -hannel changes, the rule catalog and brief re-interpretation (
          -D37–D40), and to the changes listed below.                    
      211 -4. **Implementation.** The colours, spacing, radii and type sc
          -ale are the CSS tokens in `web/src/styles.css`. Each plan task
          - builds its screens from the matching boards (T15 sign-in and 
          -shell; T16 audience; T17 content and review; T18 dashboard, li
          -ve delivery and resend).                                      
      212 -                                                              
      213 -Kept throughout: estimate vs actual always labelled; nothing s
          -ends without an explicit choice of *Send now* or *Schedule* an
          -d a second confirmation; one status vocabulary; mock mode alwa
          -ys visible; labelled fields with errors next to them, visible 
          -focus and keyboard-usable menus.                              
      214 -                                                              
      215 -Changes from the design so far:                               
      216 -                                                              
      217 -- **Sign in:** no demo email and password on the page and no "
          -fill" button. The design's own notes say not to hard-code them
          -, and the backend never exposes a password; the page points to
          - this README instead. The badge mentions only the mock provide
          -rs, because the AI mode is known only after sign-in.          
      218 -- **User menu:** the email under the name instead of a role, s
          -ince all marketers share one role (D10).                      
      219 -- **Language switch** (D44): the design has none; it sits abov
          -e the sign-in form and at the bottom of the sidebar.          
      220 -- **Preview people:** the design shows real-looking customers 
          -("Shown as Ayşe Demir", "longest name in this group"); the pre
          -view uses two clearly labelled fictional customers instead, an
          -d the real impact of long names comes from the backend's over-
          -limit estimate (no customer data on screen).                  
      221 -- **Approval:** the design's approve dialog and review page ar
          -e one flow here: the choice of when and the review tick sit on
          - the review page, then one confirmation dialog repeats the est
          -imates.                                                       
      222 -- **Saving drafts:** every change saves itself through the API
          - with the draft's version, so a "Saving… / All changes saved" 
          -indicator replaces the *Save draft* button and the unsaved-cha
          -nges dialog. Only the brief text waits for *Re-interpret*.    
      223 -- **Rule preview:** the design previews the count while a rule
          - is edited; counting unsaved rules would need another endpoint
          -, so the count updates when the rule is applied and shows the 
          -change ("−82 since your last edit").                          
      224 -- **Dropped notes:** "‘20% discount’ was used for the content,
          - not the audience" (the AI gives no such explanation) and "N w
          -ithout consent" (the counts already exclude anyone without con
          -sent).                                                        
      225 -- **Campaign names:** there is no separate name (D43). Lists s
          -how the brief's first ~60 characters, cut at a word with "…", 
          -and a campaign's page shows the full brief.                   
      226 -- **Building rules without the AI** for a new campaign is not 
          -offered yet: an unreadable brief is rephrased.                
      227 -- **Rule editor:** a modal dialog instead of the design's popo
          -ver beside the chip. A dialog keeps focus inside it, closes wi
          -th Escape and returns focus to the chip, and fits narrow scree
          -ns.                                                           
      228 -- **Breadcrumb:** "Campaigns / New campaign" is replaced by a 
          -back button and the page title.                               
      229 -- **Rules help text** reads "Click a rule to edit it" rather t
          -han "Suggested by Mock AI", which stops being true once the ma
          -rketer edits a rule.                                          
      230 -                                                              
      231 -- **"Uncertain"** (D46): in the backend it is a flag on a mess
          -age (D5), not a status. The screens show it as its own group: 
          -a message that ended without being sent (failed, skipped or ca
          -ncelled) after an attempt that may have reached the provider. 
          -A sent message counts as sent.                                
      232 -- **429 counts:** shown for the last 5 minutes, as designed, a
          -nd in total.                                                  
      233 -- **Recipient details** (D47): contacts are masked by the serv
          -er, and the search covers names only, never phone numbers or e
          -mails. The panel is a snapshot with its time, as designed.    
      234 -- **Resend** (D48): the design edits and approves the shorter 
          -SMS in one dialog. Here the dialog shows who gets the resend a
          -nd who doesn't, then creates a draft that goes through the con
          -tent and review steps like any campaign (D31), with the origin
          -al message viewable. Offered on any channel with length skips 
          -once the campaign has finished.                               
      235 -- **Dashboard rows** don't mention resend opportunities; the c
          -ampaign's page does. The campaign list searches the brief, sin
          -ce there are no names (D43); search, filters and paging run on
          - the server (D54).                                            
      236 -- **Archive:** not built (deferred; see Future improvements).

      238 -## Test                                                       
      239 -                                                              
      240 -```sh                                                         
      241 -make test         # in Docker: throwaway Postgres + Go tests, 
          -with Vitest and the frontend type check alongside             
      242 -make test-local   # needs local Go and DATABASE_URL pointing t
          -o a Postgres (Go tests only)                                  
      243 -make lint         # in Docker: gofmt check + go vet           
      244 -make e2e          # browser tests (Playwright) on their own th
          -rowaway stack                                                 
      245 -```                                                           
      246 -                                                              
      247 -`make test` runs the Go tests and the frontend checks at the s
          -ame time; the frontend's output is printed after Go's. Each Go
          - test gets its own database, copied from a template that is mi
          -grated once per run (much faster than migrating each one), and
          - the `db` and `e2e` packages run their tests in parallel. Fron
          -tend packages are reinstalled only when the lockfile changes. 
          -On this project's reference machine (Docker Desktop, 8 CPUs) a
          - run takes about a minute; see `docs/performance.md`.         
      248 -                                                              
      249 -`make e2e` runs the browser suite in `e2e/` in Microsoft's Pla
          -ywright image (pinned to 1.64.1; nothing is added to the app) 
          -on **its own stack** (D53): a separate Compose project (`scc-e
          -2e`, `docker-compose.e2e.yml`) with a fresh, seeded database, 
          -mock AI only and a 2-second cap on retry waits (`RETRY_BACKOFF
          -_MAX`; the real 30-second cap stays covered by the Go tests). 
          -It starts the stack, runs the checks, and removes the stack, s
          -o your local app and its data are never touched and nothing ha
          -s to be running first. The dashboard check runs first and alon
          -e (it compares counts over all campaigns); then the checks tha
          -t send nothing and the checks that send run side by side, the 
          -sending ones one after another so they don't share the provide
          -r's 100/s limit. Each file's time is printed at the end.      
      250 -                                                              
      251 -What it covers: sign-in and the login guard, the audience step
          -, autosave races (slowed and failing saves, switching campaign
          -s, changes made elsewhere), English and Turkish, the content a
          -nd review steps (streaming and Stop, no lost edits when leavin
          -g, conflicts during generation, explicit approval), the dashbo
          -ard and campaign list (status counts, filters and search in th
          -e address, paging across several pages with a filter and a sea
          -rch, delete and cancel with a confirmation), layouts at deskto
          -p, tablet and phone widths (no sideways scrolling, the account
          - menu in reach, campaign rows readable in English and Turkish)
          - and signing out with the keyboard, the campaign page after ap
          -proval (a scheduled campaign and its cancel; live delivery to 
          -completion, with counts checked against the API), recipient de
          -tails (tabs, search, paging, attempt history, masked contacts,
          - Back, Escape and focus, Turkish), resend (the dialog's number
          -s, the draft through content and review, an audience of exactl
          -y the eligible skipped recipients, links both ways, a resend c
          -reated elsewhere), a send watched mid-way (counts moving, paus
          -e and resume) and cancelled while sending (sent messages stay 
          -sent, the rest are cancelled, nothing is sent afterwards), and
          - the fixes from the PR #6 review. Several checks really send c
          -ampaigns through the mock SMS provider of that stack. A full r
          -un takes under two minutes. Set `SHOTS=/e2e/shots` to keep scr
          -eenshots.                                                     
      252 -                                                              
      253 -`make perf` measures the performance targets (D57) on another 
          -throwaway stack (`scc-perf`, `docker-compose.perf.yml`, defaul
          -t settings): a full send watched by 20 live screens, 10 market
          -ers using the API for 2 minutes, and cold loads and interactio
          -ns in a browser on a desktop and a throttled phone profile. It
          - prints each target with what was measured and takes about 5 m
          -inutes. Results and method: `docs/performance.md`.

      255 -### Continuous integration (D52)                              
      256 -                                                              
      257 -GitHub Actions (`.github/workflows/`):                        
      258 -                                                              
      259 -| Workflow | When | What |                                    
      260 -|---|---|---|                                                 
      261 -| **CI** (`ci.yml`) | every push and every pull request, and o
          -n request | `make test`, `make lint`, and the scan: `make scan
          --selftest`, the files in the repository, and the new commits |
      262 -| **Browser checks** (`e2e.yml`) | every pull request, every p
          -ush to `main`, nightly at 02:30 UTC, and on request (Actions →
          - Browser checks → Run workflow) | `make e2e` on its own stack;
          - screenshots of failing files are kept for 14 days |          
      263 -                                                              
      264 -Nothing runs twice for the same commit: a push to a branch wit
          -h an open pull request is left to the pull request's run, whic
          -h tests the commit merged with `main`, and a newer commit canc
          -els the older run. The jobs run the same make targets as a dev
          -eloper's machine, in Docker. In CI the Go and npm caches live 
          -in the workspace (`.github/ci/compose.cache.yml`), so later ru
          -ns reuse them. Actions are pinned to commits. The repository i
          -s private, so the runs use the account's Actions minutes (abou
          -t 4 runner-minutes for CI and 4 for the browser checks per pul
          -l request update).

      266 -### Scanning for secrets and personal data (D51)              
      267 -                                                              
      268 -```sh                                                         
      269 -make scan           # the files Git sees and the commits not p
          -ushed yet                                                     
      270 -make scan-selftest  # proves the rules on planted keys, emails
          -, phone numbers and a .env file                               
      271 -make hooks          # once per clone: scan the staged changes 
          -before every commit                                           
      272 -```                                                           
      273 -                                                              
      274 -`make scan` runs [gitleaks](https://github.com/gitleaks/gitlea
          -ks) (pinned Docker image, nothing installed) with gitleaks' ow
          -n rules plus the project's rules in `.gitleaks.toml`: Anthropi
          -c keys, email addresses, phone numbers and committed `.env` fi
          -les. Only the project's fictional seed and test data, placehol
          -ders, the documented demo login and masked contacts are allowe
          -d; anything else blocks the commit, with the value redacted in
          - the output. Session exports in `ai-sessions/` are reviewed be
          -fore they are committed and genuine secrets or private details
          - are redacted; the owner's public GitHub username and profile 
          -links are kept. Exports may quote the scanner self-test's plan
          -ted fictional contacts, which are allowed only there. `scripts
          -/scan.sh history` scans every commit, as a report.            
      275 -                                                              
      276 -Integration tests fail, never skip, when no database is availa
          -ble. `internal/e2e` runs the API, the worker and the mock prov
          -ider together on a seeded database: a send through a provider 
          -that fails, rate-limits and loses responses, a scheduled send,
          - and an over-limit send followed by a resend. Its lifecycle te
          -sts cover timeouts and replays, worker shutdowns and crashes, 
          -cancellation races, consent changes, length skips, resends aft
          -er a cancelled resend, retry limits and two workers on one que
          -ue (the second waits for the sender lock).                    
      277 -                                                              
      278 -Performance of the recipient API and the status stream on 3,00
          -0 customers, with the benchmark that measures them and the que
          -ry plans behind the changes: `docs/performance.md`.           
      279 -                                                              
      280 -The first run compiles all dependencies and takes a few minute
          -s; compiled packages are kept in the `gobuildcache` volume (an
          -d in BuildKit cache mounts for image builds), so later runs ta
          -ke seconds. `docker compose down -v` also clears these caches.
      281 -                                                              
      282 -## Architecture (current)                                     
      283 -                                                              
      284 -One Go module (`cmd/scc`) built into one image and run as sepa
          -rate Compose services (D20): `postgres`, `migrate` (one-shot: 
          -migrations + seed), `api`, `worker`, `mock-provider`. `interna
          -l/config` reads environment variables, `internal/obs` serves h
          -ealth and metrics and counts HTTP requests, `internal/db` hold
          -s the embedded migrations and the seed, `internal/auth` the lo
          -gin, logout and session check. `internal/audience` validates a
          -udience rules against a fixed catalog (D14) and turns them int
          -o parameterized SQL; rule values only ever reach Postgres as q
          -uery arguments. `internal/provider` is the mock provider. `int
          -ernal/worker` holds the queue (the messages table, claimed wit
          -h `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` and a 30 s lease, D5, D18) and the 
          -dispatcher. `internal/spa` serves the built frontend. `interna
          -l/content` validates message templates (only `{{first_name}}`,
          - `{{last_name}}`, `{{city}}`; SMS ≤ 160, subject ≤ 80, email b
          -ody ≤ 2,000 characters after personalization) and estimates ov
          -er-limit messages per channel and language in SQL; it also hol
          -ds the language catalog (D64).                                
      285 -                                                              
      286 -## Optional features                                          
      287 -                                                              
      288 -- **Scheduled sending:** built (D29).                         
      289 -- **Messages in the customer's language:** built (T20, D64–D67
          -); see the content step above.                                
      290 -- **A/B testing:** built as a delivery-only comparison (T21, D
          -68, superseding D65); see the content step above.             
      291 -- **CI pipeline:** built (D52).                               
      292 -                                                              
      293 -## Future improvements                                        
      294 -                                                              
      295 -- **More languages and AI cost:** the design already supports 
          -languages beyond Turkish and English: the catalog, per-languag
          -e content, counts and review. Adding one is a catalog entry, a
          - migration and mock texts. Before expanding, weigh the AI cost
          -: each language adds a full set of messages to the AI's answer
          -, and output tokens are most of the cost (`docs/plans/2026-10-
          -24-t20-ai-cost.md`). Options: keep generating only the selecte
          -d languages, reuse approved content across campaigns, record t
          -okens per generation, and use a cheaper model for content afte
          -r a quality check.                                            
      296 -                                                              
      297 -- **A/B outcomes:** the stored version per message is what a r
          -eal outcome would join on. The cheapest is orders placed withi
          -n an attribution window after `sent_at` (the `orders` table al
          -ready exists; needs a decision on the window), then tracked li
          -nks for clicks. A winner needs one of these plus a significanc
          -e rule (D68).                                                 
      298 -- **AI-generated campaign titles:** the AI could suggest a sho
          -rt title in the same answer that interprets the brief, with th
          -e brief's start as the fallback and manual edits kept. Deferre
          -d to keep the scope small (D43).                              
      299 -- **API reference (OpenAPI and Swagger UI), a final UI/UX audi
          -t and a product roadmap:** planned for T21 (D50) but not built
          -; the API is described in this README and the decisions.      
      300 -- **Archiving campaigns:** hide finished campaigns from the li
          -st. Not built: deleting drafts removes most of the clutter, an
          -d archiving can be added later without changing how deletion w
          -orks (D37).                                                   
      301 -                                                              
      119  ## Security
      120  
      304 -The security review (T19) with its evidence is in `docs/securi
          -ty-review.md`. In short: customer contacts are masked by the s
          -erver and never cached; the AI never receives customer data; e
          -very API route needs a session; failed sign-ins are limited (5
          - per 15 minutes per email and per address); requests from othe
          -r sites are refused; the app is served with a strict Content S
          -ecurity Policy; secrets and personal data are scanned before e
          -very commit and in CI.                                        
      121 +Details in [`docs/security-review.md`](docs/security-review.md
          +). Contacts are masked by the server and never cached; the AI 
          +never receives customer data; every API route except login nee
          +ds a session (HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax cookie; only the token's 
          +hash is stored); failed sign-ins are limited; writes from othe

      122  
      123  ## Known limitations
      124  
      308 -- The streams of a campaign share one status snapshot (D59): i
          -ts database cost grows with the campaigns being watched, not w
          -ith the screens (20 screens on one send: about 25 ms of databa
          -se time per second, see `docs/performance.md`). Nothing limits
          - how many streams are open, and the sharing is per api process
          -.                                                             
      309 -- If no worker is running, a cancelled campaign's stream stays
          - open until its in-flight messages are reclaimed.             
      310 -- Metrics count per process since it started.                 
      311 -- A/B report: delivery data only, so it can't show which versi
          -on works better (D68). The split is exact per language over un
          -ique recipients; per channel it can differ slightly, since con
          -sent and contact details differ. Content is fixed by the API o
          -nce approved; a direct database edit would change what retries
          - send.                                                        
      312 -- A/B estimate: version B's over-limit count before sending co
          -vers the whole audience, though about half gets B, so it is an
          - upper bound. Recipient details (P5) miss the 50 ms p95 target
          - back to back: 55.4 ms in one run after the audit fixes (D69),
          - on a host the test saturates. One request costs about 9 ms; t
          -he rest is waiting behind the whole request mix, where the cam
          -paign list's queries use the most database time. With a 1 s pa
          -use it measured 45 ms in T19, not re-measured since (see `docs
          -/performance.md`, Q6).                                        
      313 -- Recipient details page by offset (D47): while a campaign sen
          -ds, rows can move between pages, and the panel shows a snapsho
          -t with its time. Sorting by the stored last attempt (D69) is a
          - top-N sort of the group, about 5 ms for a 5,000-message group
          -; an index on it is the upgrade path.                         
      314 -- After a campaign's status stream has ended, a resend created
          - in another tab is noticed when the page is opened again or a 
          -resend fails (D36).                                           
      315 -- The Inter font from the design is not bundled: the app uses 
          -Inter when it is installed and the system font otherwise. Ther
          -e is no frontend linter yet; `make test` runs the TypeScript t
          -ype check.                                                    
      316 -- Sign-in limits are kept in memory per api process and reset 
          -on restart; behind a proxy, all clients share its address.    
      317 -- Real AI calls are limited to `AI_CALLS_PER_HOUR` (default 10
          -) per marketer in any hour, retries included (D63); over the l
          -imit the API answers `429` with `Retry-After`. The limit is ke
          -pt in memory per api process and resets on restart; the Anthro
          -pic console's spend limit is the real cost ceiling. Mock AI is
          - unlimited.                                                   
      125 +- **Not built:** the final UI/UX audit and the product roadmap
          + planned for T21 (D50); AI-generated campaign titles (D43); ar
          +chiving campaigns (D37); A/B outcomes such as orders or clicks
          + (D68).                                                       
      126 +- **`/docs`** loads Swagger UI from the jsDelivr CDN (pinned, 
          +integrity-checked), so it needs internet access in the browser
          +; the spec at `/docs/openapi.json` works offline. The spec is 
          +written by hand: a test keeps its routes in step with the serv
          +er, not every field.                                          
      127 +- **Per-process state:** sign-in limits and the AI call limit 
          +live in memory per api process and reset on restart; behind a 
          +proxy, all clients share its address. Metrics count per proces
          +s.                                                            
      128 +- **Status streams** share one snapshot per campaign (D59), bu
          +t nothing limits how many are open. Without a running worker, 
          +a cancelled campaign's stream stays open until in-flight messa
          +ges are reclaimed. A resend created in another tab after a str
          +eam ended shows on reload (D36).                              
      129 +- **Recipient details** page by offset, so rows can move while
          + a campaign sends (D47). Under back-to-back load they measured
          + 55.4 ms p95 against a 50 ms target (`docs/performance.md`, Q6
          +).

      635  - **Trade-offs:** A failed lock connection is noticed within a
           bout 1–3 s; until then, if the database already freed the lock
           , a second worker could send at the same time. A worker that t
           akes the lock waits one cap window (about 1 s) before sending,
            so a handover can't put 200 requests in a second. A vanished 
           host keeps the lock until the server's keepalives drop its ses
           sion (about 25 s), and sending pauses meanwhile. A message fai
           led for missing text shows the reason "retries exhausted". `la
           st_attempt_at` duplicates `delivery_attempts`; only the worker
            writes attempts, and it keeps both.
      636  - **History:** 2026-10-24: approved by the engineer. 2026-10-2
           4 (branch review): the handover wait and the lock connection's
            keepalives were added.

          +UI from a CDN                                                 
      639 +- **Status:** Accepted · 2026-10-25                           
      640 +- **Decision:** An OpenAPI 3.1 spec of every `/api` route (aut
          +h, schemas, error codes), hand-written as JSON in `internal/ap
          +idocs` and embedded in the binary. The api serves it at `/docs
          +/openapi.json` and a Swagger UI page at `/docs`, both public. 
          +Swagger UI loads from jsDelivr at an exact version with SRI ha
          +shes, under a CSP for that page only; the app's CSP (D56) is u
          +nchanged. A test fails when a registered route and the spec di
          +ffer.                                                         
      641 +- **Why:** The API was described only in the README (D50 plann

          +o API behavior.                                               
      642 +- **Rejected:** *Vendoring swagger-ui-dist:* ~1.5 MB of third-
          +party files in the repo and image. *An npm dependency in the w
          +eb build:* a new dependency and build changes for a developer 
          +page. *Generating the spec from code:* annotations or a librar
          +y across every handler.                                       
      643 +- **Trade-offs:** `/docs` needs internet access in the browser
          +. The test checks routes, not every field, so response shapes

      48 -### AI mode                                                    
      39 +| Requirement | How it is met |                                
      40 +|---|---|                                                      
      41 +| Claude creates audience rules; the backend validates them; ne
         +ver run Claude's SQL | Claude returns rules as JSON against a f
         +ixed catalog (D14). Go validates field, operator and value, and
         + builds parameterized SQL itself; rule values reach Postgres on
         +ly as query arguments. |                                       
      42 +| Show matching users; SMS and email permissions checked separa
         +tely | Drafts show the audience per channel, counted from `sms_
         +opt_in` / `email_opt_in`. The worker re-checks the recipient's 
         +permission for that channel before every send. |               
      43 +| Claude writes SMS and email, streamed to the UI | Server-sent
         + events stream the text into the editors as it is written (D21)
         +. |                                                            
      44 +| SMS ≤ 160, subject ≤ 80, variables, preview and edit before a
         +pproval | Limits are checked after personalization with `{{firs
         +t_name}}`, `{{last_name}}`, `{{city}}`; recipients over the lim
         +it are skipped and counted. The marketer edits, previews and ap
         +proves each language. |                                        
      45 +| Asynchronous queue, ≤ 100 messages/s per channel | The `messa
         +ges` table is the queue, claimed with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` 
         +and leases (D5, D18). Per channel, a rate limiter plus a hard c
         +ap over each 1-second window, both counting retries (D19, D69).
         + |                                                             
      46 +| No duplicate messages | One message per campaign, user and ch
         +annel (unique constraint). The message ID is the provider's ide
         +mpotency key, so a retry after a lost response is not sent twic
         +e. |                                                           
      47 +| Retry strategy | 429, 5xx, timeouts and network errors are re
         +tried with exponential backoff and jitter, honouring `Retry-Aft
         +er`, up to `MAX_ATTEMPTS` (5); other 4xx fail. Each attempt is 
         +recorded. |                                                    
      48 +| Mock SMS and email providers with success, delays, temporary 
         +errors and 429 | The `mock-provider` service: 20–200 ms delay, 
         +5% 5xx, 3% 429, 1% lost responses by default, configurable per 
         +channel (D20). |                                               
      49 +| Live dashboard status, metrics, health checks | A per-campaig
         +n status stream (D16, D36); `/metrics` (D26), `/healthz` and `/
         +readyz` on every process. |                                    
      50 +| Structured AI JSON, validated, invalid output handled safely 
         +| See [Reliable AI design](#reliable-ai-design). |             
      51 +| Tests without an API key; key from `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in `.e
         +nv`; `.env.example` | Every test uses the mock LLM or a fake lo
         +cal API server. The key is read only from the environment. |   
      52 +| 3,000 fictional users with names, email, phone, city, orders,
         + cart, permissions | Seeded once by the `migrate` service; rest
         +arts never duplicate them (D28). |                             
      53 +| Vue.js frontend, Go backend | Vue 3 + TypeScript + Vite (D25)
         +; one Go module (D20). |                                       
      54 +| Deliverables: `docker compose up`, `make test`, README, `DECI
         +SIONS.md`, `ai-sessions/` | All present. Session exports are re
         +viewed for keys and personal data before they are committed (D5
         +1). |

      56 +## Bonus features                                              
      57  
      58 +- **Scheduled sending (D29):** approval requires an explicit *S
         +end now* or a time between 1 minute and 30 days ahead. A time f
         +rom the brief is only a proposal. The worker starts the campaig
         +n at that time and builds the audience then (D30), and it can b
         +e cancelled until then.                                        
      59 +- **Messages in the customer's language (D64–D67):** **Turkish 
         +and English are implemented.** Each customer gets the text in t
         +heir own language if the campaign has it, and the default langu
         +age otherwise. Each language is reviewed and approved on its ow
         +n; a changed brief marks the text "may be outdated". The langua
         +ge list is one catalog in `internal/content/languages.go` that 
         +drives the API, validation, the AI's answer schema and the UI. 
         +A further language would still need a migration widening the da
         +tabase's language checks, seed data and mock texts; a test fail
         +s while the catalog and the database checks disagree. No other 
         +language works today.                                          
      60 +- **A/B test (D68):** optional and off by default. Version B pe
         +r language is written by hand or by the AI. Each language's rec
         +ipients are split 50/50 by `md5(campaign:user)`, which is deter
         +ministic and the same on every channel, and the version is stor
         +ed on each message. Results show delivery per version (sent, fa
         +iled, skipped, pending). There are **no clicks, conversions, st
         +atistical significance or winner selection**: the app records n
         +o outcomes after delivery.                                     
      61 +- **CI (D52):** GitHub Actions runs `make test`, `make lint` an
         +d the secret scan on every push and pull request, and the brows
         +er suite on pull requests, on pushes to `main` and nightly. Act
         +ions are pinned to commits.

       82  
       68 -- **Brief → audience:** the AI maps the brief to rules from a 
          -fixed catalog (city, cart and order history, spend, categories
          -, sign-up date; D14, D70), or says it can't. Rules are validat
          -ed and shown as editable chips with live counts per channel; t
          -hey can also be built by hand.                                
       69 -- **Content:** the AI streams SMS and email text per language 
          -(Turkish, English) into editors; text is personalised with `{{
          -first_name}}`, `{{last_name}}`, `{{city}}` and length-checked 
          -after personalisation (SMS 160, subject 80, email body 2,000).
          - Each language is reviewed and approved on its own (D64–D67). 
          -Edits save automatically with optimistic locking.             
       70 -- **Approval:** nothing is sent without an explicit *Send now*
          - or *Schedule* (1 minute to 30 days ahead) and a second confir
          -mation; estimates are labelled as estimates (D30).            
       71 -- **Sending:** the worker builds the audience at send time, re
          --checks consent and cancellation before each request, sends wi
          -th an idempotency key, retries 429/5xx/timeouts with backoff, 
          -and stays within 100 requests per second per channel (D19, D69
          -). Over-limit messages are skipped and can be resent (D31).   
       72 -- **Monitoring:** a live status stream per campaign (sent, unc
          -ertain, failed, skipped, cancelled, retrying, pending, with re
          -asons), recipient details with masked contacts (D46, D47), can
          -cel and resend.                                               
       73 -- **A/B test (optional):** version B per language, a reproduci
          -ble 50/50 split, delivery results per version; no winner is na
          -med, since no outcome data exists (D68).                      
       74 -- **Dashboard and list:** status counts, server-side search, f
          -ilters and paging (D54). English and Turkish UI (D44).

       96 +## Reliable AI design                                         
       97 +                                                              
       98 +- **Structured JSON:** both calls use Anthropic structured out
          +put with a JSON schema. Field and operator are fixed lists fro
          +m the catalog; content has one object per requested language a
          +nd channel.                                                   
       99 +- **Strict validation in Go:** the answer is decoded strictly 
          +(unknown fields and trailing data rejected). Rules are checked
          + against the catalog and its limits and normalized. Content mu
          +st cover exactly the requested languages and channels, fit the
          + length limits after personalization, and use only known varia
          +bles. AI-written bodies must greet with `{{first_name}}` (D72)
          +.                                                             
      100 +- **Safe SQL:** Claude never writes SQL. Go builds each rule's
          + SQL from a fixed template, with values passed only as query a
          +rguments.                                                     
      101 +- **Controlled retries:** an invalid answer is retried **once*
          +*, with the validation errors sent back. A second failure retu
          +rns `502 ai_invalid`; provider errors return `502 ai_unavailab
          +le`, timeouts `504`. Only a valid answer is saved, and streame
          +d content only while the client is still connected (D15). Real
          + calls are capped per marketer (D63).                         
      102 +- **Unsupported handling:** the prompt and the mock both answe
          +r `unsupported` with a reason when any part of the audience ca
          +n't be expressed. The brief is treated as data inside `<brief>
          +` tags, never as instructions. Nothing is saved, and the marke
          +ter can rephrase.                                             
      103 +- **Mock LLM:** tests and the default mode use a deterministic
          + mock, with the same interface and validation path as Claude. 
          +The Claude client is tested against a fake local API server.  
      104 +

      158  ## Security
      159  
      121 -Details in [`docs/security-review.md`](docs/security-review.md
          -). Contacts are masked by the server and never cached; the AI 
          -never receives customer data; every API route except login nee
          -ds a session (HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax cookie; only the token's 
          -hash is stored); failed sign-ins are limited; writes from othe
          -r sites are refused; the app has a strict Content Security Pol
          -icy; secrets and personal data are scanned before every commit
          - and in CI. The session cookie isn't `Secure` on plain `http:/
          -/localhost`.                                                  
      160 +Details in [`docs/security-review.md`](docs/security-review.md
          +). Contacts are masked by the server and never cached. The AI 
          +never receives customer data. Every API route except login nee
          +ds a session (HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax cookie; only the token's

⏺ The interleaved before/after perf runs (3 pairs, about 25 minutes) are running
  in the background. I'll report as soon as they finish.

  So far:
  - Baseline: current main measured P5 at 46.2, 56.0, 64.2 and 79.0 ms p95 over
    four runs, while host load ranged from 4 to 38.
  - Cause: every recipient request scans the campaign's full 6,000 messages two
    or three times, even for small groups. Waiting for CPU behind the other
    requests stretches those scans into the p95 tail. The connection pool is not
    the bottleneck.
  - Fix: two query-only changes in internal/campaign/recipients.go. The list now
    filters on status before the display group, so small groups can use the
    existing (campaign_id, status) index. The skipped group's reasons are
    counted from the stored skip reason, without attempt lookups. On the
    database alone, average recipient work per request drops from about 6.3 to
    about 4.3 ms. No index, no schema change, and the test and target are
    unchanged.
  - Test: I added one test covering all seven groups. It passed before and after
    the change, and it fails when I deliberately break the status mapping.
  - README: the limitations section is regrouped into A/B, languages and
    scalability. The "not built" list is gone, and the Inter font and linter
    notes moved to a new "Future technical improvements" section. The P5 line
    waits for the results.

  Nothing is committed or pushed.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ P5 is better but not reliably under 50 ms on this laptop: after the fix it
  measured 38.6, 52.2 and 33.3 ms p95, with one miss during a host load spike.
  Everything is local; nothing is committed, pushed or added to PR #17.

  Bottleneck: every recipient request scanned all 6,000 messages of the test
  campaign two or three times, even when the requested group held only 300 or
  480 rows. Under 10 back-to-back marketers, those scans wait for CPU behind the
  other requests, which is what pushes the p95 up. Recipient details took about
  a quarter of the api's database time. The connection pool was not the problem
  (at most 5 of 6 open connections busy, below the cap of 8).

  Fix (two query changes in internal/campaign/recipients.go, no index or schema
  change):
  - The list now filters on the group's message statuses first, so small groups
    use the existing (campaign_id, status) index instead of reading the whole
    campaign.
  - For the skipped group, the reason counts come straight from the stored skip
    reason, with no lookups into delivery attempts. That query went from 4.6 to
    0.48 ms.
  - Measured directly in the database, the average work per request drops from
    about 6.3 to 4.3 ms.
  - I measured a covering index for the group counts too (2.7 → 2.0 ms) and left
    it out. It would add a write on every message for 0.7 ms.

  Before/after (the same 10-marketer benchmark from make perf, with the same
  settings, the two code versions run alternately on the same machine):

      115 +## API reference and error handling                           
      116 +                                                              
      117 +The full reference is at [`/docs`](http://localhost:8080/docs)
          + (Swagger UI) and [`/docs/openapi.json`](http://localhost:8080
          +/docs/openapi.json) (OpenAPI 3.1). Every `/api` route except `
          +POST /api/login` needs the session cookie set by login. Succes
          +sful calls answer `200`, `201` (campaign or resend created) or
          + `204` (draft deleted, logged out). Errors are JSON: `{"error"
          +: "..."}`, with `code`, `field_errors`, `unsupported` or `retr
          +y_after_seconds` where noted.                                 
      118 +                                                              
      119 +| HTTP | `code` | Meaning | Expected behavior |               
      120 +|---|---|---|---|                                             
      121 +| 400 | — | The body isn't one JSON object of the documented s
          +hape: unknown fields, trailing data, or over 64 KiB (4 KiB for
          + login). At login: email or password missing | Fix the request
          + |                                                            
      122 +| 401 | — | No valid session (`login required`); at login, a w
          +rong email or password (one answer for both) | Sign in again |
      123 +| 403 | — | A POST, PUT or DELETE whose `Origin` is another si
          +te | Call from the app's own origin |                         
      124 +| 404 | — | The campaign doesn't exist (or the id isn't a posi
          +tive integer); the campaign has no text in that language | Rel
          +oad the list |                                                
      125 +| 409 | — | The draft changed since it was loaded (stale `vers
          +ion`); the status doesn't allow it (only drafts are edited, de
          +leted or approved; only approved or sending campaigns are canc
          +elled); the text exists and `replace` wasn't set; resend confl
          +icts | Reload, or confirm the replacement |                   
      126 +| 422 | — | Invalid values: rules (`field_errors` with the rul
          +e's index), content (`field_errors` with the field, e.g. `en.s
          +ms.body`), channels, time zone, send time, query parameters, m
          +issing `version`, missing text or approvals, an empty audience
          + | Correct the input |                                        
      127 +| 422 | — | The AI can't express the brief with the rule catal
          +og: `"unsupported": true` and the AI's reason. Nothing is save
          +d | Rephrase the brief or build the rules by hand |           
      128 +| 429 | — | Too many failed sign-ins: 5 per 15 minutes per ema
          +il and per client address; `Retry-After` header | Wait, then r
          +etry |                                                        
      129 +| 429 | `ai_call_limit` | Real AI only: the marketer's `AI_CAL
          +LS_PER_HOUR` limit is reached; `Retry-After` and `retry_after_
          +seconds` | Wait; editing by hand still works |                
      130 +| 500 | — | Unexpected failure: `internal error`, details logg
          +ed only | Retry later |                                       
      131 +| 502 | `ai_invalid` | Creating a draft or re-interpreting a b
          +rief: the AI's answer failed validation twice. Nothing is save
          +d | Rephrase, or build the rules by hand |                    
      132 +| 502 | `ai_unavailable` | Creating a draft or re-interpreting
          + a brief: the AI provider failed (bad key, outage) | Retry lat
          +er, or build the rules by hand |                              
      133 +| 504 | — | Creating a draft or re-interpreting a brief: the A
          +I didn't answer within `LLM_TIMEOUT` | Retry |                
      134 +                                                              
      135 +Message generation (`POST /api/campaigns/{id}/content`) answer
          +s the checks above (400, 404, 409, 422, 429) before streaming.
          + After that, failures arrive as an `error` event: `{"message",
          + "code"?}`, with `ai_invalid` or `ai_unavailable` as above. A 
          +refusal, a timeout, the AI call limit reached during a retry, 
          +a draft changed meanwhile or an internal error carries a messa
          +ge without a code. Nothing is saved unless the answer is valid
          +.

      370  - **Status:** Accepted · 2026-10-21
      371  - **Decision:** No separate broker. Workers claim batches with
            `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`: messages that are `pending` or `ret
           ry_scheduled` and due, or `sending` with an expired lease. Res
           ult updates only apply while the worker still holds the lease,
            so a stale worker cannot overwrite a newer result.
      372  - **Why:** The lease, attempt count and next-attempt time (D5,
            D7) already live in this table. No dual write between a datab
           ase and a broker, no outbox, no extra service. At most 200 mes
           sages/s is light load for Postgres.

          -. *RabbitMQ or NATS JetStream:* extra service, dual writes, aw
          -kward delayed retries. *River:* its job table duplicates our m
          -essage state machine.

⏺ I added the explanation to the README's Architecture section and one entry to
  D18 in DECISIONS.md. Nothing is committed or pushed.

  DECISIONS.md: the choice was already recorded, so I kept the existing
  decisions:
  - D18 ("The messages table is the queue") rejects Redis + asynq and RabbitMQ /
    NATS JetStream, with reasons and trade-offs.
  - D59 rejects Redis or Kafka for the live status streams.
  - D69 rejects a shared Redis rate limiter.

  The one gap was Kafka as the message queue, which no decision named. I added
  it to D18's rejected alternatives: an extra cluster and dual writes, and a log
  with no per-message delayed retry or lease, so retries would still need the
  table. A dated history note says the decision itself is unchanged.

  README: a short paragraph under Architecture says why the messages table is
  the queue. A message's status, lease, attempts and next retry live in one row,
  so there are no dual writes and no extra service, and 200 messages/s is light
  load for Postgres. It points to D18, D59 and D69 rather than repeating their
  reasoning. Three bullets say when each technology would start to pay off:
  - Redis: several api replicas sharing sign-in and AI-call limits, a cache, or
    pub/sub for live status.
  - RabbitMQ or NATS: several independent sending services, per-provider queues
    or priorities, or push delivery instead of polling.
  - Kafka: a durable stream of delivery events for analytics and A/B outcomes,
    or volumes beyond one database's write rate.

  The scaling bullets are general guidance, not plans recorded in DECISIONS.md.
  The README diff also still holds the earlier uncommitted changes (API error
  table, completion summary, setup, QA and lint wording). Everything is ready to
  commit to PR #17 when you ask.

           . The test checks routes, not every field, so response shapes 
           can drift unnoticed. The page allows inline styles, which Swag
           ger UI needs.
