› Message from general-purpose
  ## Frontend review: web/src (every non-test file read in full; no files
  edited; tests not run)

  I found 4 real defects and 1 low-priority hardening item. I dropped anything I
  couldn't trace to a concrete failure.

  ### 1. MEDIUM: an expired session during delivery monitoring never redirects
  to sign-in
  - **Where:** `web/src/components/DeliveryStatus.vue:57`,
  `web/src/api.ts:15,47`
  - **Scenario:** the marketer is watching a sending campaign and the session
  expires. EventSource gets a 401 on reconnect and closes, so `onClosed` calls
  `api('/api/me')` to "send an expired session to sign-in" (the comment at line
  56). But `/api/me` is in `sessionPaths`, so `responseError` deliberately skips
  `onUnauthorized()` for it. The 401 is swallowed by `.catch(() => {})`. The
  page shows "connection closed" with a Retry button that fails the same way
  every time, and the redirect never happens.
  - **How I verified it:** traced `responseError`: `if (res.status === 401 &&
  !sessionPaths.includes(path)) onUnauthorized()`. `api.test.ts:62-68` confirms
  that a 401 from `/api/me` is meant to be silent. There are no component tests
  for DeliveryStatus.
  - **Smallest fix:** probe a non-session endpoint instead, e.g.
  `campaigns.get(props.campaign.id).catch(() => {})`. A 401 there triggers the
  router's unauthorized handler.

  ### 2. MEDIUM: double-clicking "Write with AI" starts two generations, and
  Stop can no longer stop the first
  - **Where:** `web/src/views/ContentView.vue:333-347` (button at 516)
  - **Scenario:** there is unsaved text (common: clicking the button blurs a
  field, which starts a save). `write()` first runs `await commit(true)`, a
  network save. During that save `streaming` is still false and the button is
  still enabled, so a second click calls `write()` again.
    - Both calls queue a `saver.save(stream)`.
    - `stop` is overwritten by the second `AbortController`, so Stop only aborts
  the queued second stream. The running first stream can't be stopped, even
  though D34 says a stopped generation saves nothing.
    - The second stream then runs with `replace: false` for languages that now
  have text. That is either an extra AI call or an error.
    - The first call's `finally` sets `streaming = false` while the second is
  still running.
  - **How I verified it:** traced the paths through `askToWrite` → `write` →
  `commit` and `saver.save`. I didn't reproduce it in a browser.
  - **Smallest fix:** add a guard at the top of `write()`: `if (starting)
  return; starting = true`, and reset it in `finally`. Alternatively, disable
  the button while a write is pending.

  ### 3. LOW-MEDIUM: checkboxes stay in a state the page didn't apply
  - **Where:** `web/src/views/ContentView.vue:485-487` (language checkbox) and
  `:496` (A/B switch)
  - **Cause:** the `:checked` binding plus `@change` isn't a two-way binding.
  When the handler leaves the state unchanged, the bound value doesn't change,
  so Vue never patches the DOM. I confirmed this in `runtime-core` `patchProps`:
  it only patches when `next !== prev`.
  - **Scenario A:** untick a saved language, then press Cancel in the remove
  dialog. The checkbox stays unticked while the language and its tab remain.
  - **Scenario B:** tick the A/B switch while a field is incomplete, or when the
  save fails. `commit()` returns false, so `setAB` returns early. The switch
  shows "on" while A/B is off, so the next click sends `enabled: false` when the
  user meant to turn it on.
  - **Smallest fix:** after the action settles, set `(e.target as
  HTMLInputElement).checked` back to the real state (`!!drafts[l]` or `abTest`),
  or use `v-model` with a computed getter and setter.

  ### 4. LOW: an older estimate response can overwrite a newer one
  - **Where:** `web/src/views/ContentView.vue:92-94` (called at 165 and 389)
  - **Scenario:** two quick saves happen, e.g. one on blur and then another edit
  and blur. Each runs `refreshEstimate()` after its save. The calls aren't
  sequenced, so if the first estimate comes back last, the "N recipients over
  the limit" warning reflects the older text.
  - **How I verified it:** saves are serialized but estimate calls aren't; there
  is no sequence check. `RecipientsDrawer` and `CampaignsView` already use a
  `seq` guard for exactly this.
  - **Smallest fix:** add the same `let seq` guard: `const mine = ++seq; const e
  = await …; if (mine === seq) estimate.value = e`.

  ### 5. LOW (hardening, needs a malformed server event): SSE payloads are cast,
  not checked
  - **Where:** `web/src/views/ContentView.vue:361-369`,
  `web/src/delivery/stream.ts:16`
  - **Scenario:** an event such as `data: null` makes `data.field` or
  `data.message` throw. The error escapes `streamContent` without cancelling the
  reader, so the HTTP stream and the server-side generation keep running. On
  the delivery side, a snapshot without `channels` throws inside the
  `deliverySummary` computed.
  - **Note:** the server is our own Go code, so this is not a likely failure.
  - **Smallest fix:** in ContentView, use `const data = (e.data ?? {}) as …`. In
  `streamContent`, call `reader.cancel()` in a `try/finally`.

  ### Unused exports
  There is no unused runtime code. 17 exported types are only used inside their
  own file (`WriteRequest`, `DashboardHint`, `SaveState`, `GenerationOutcome`,
  `Vars`, `SmsInfo`, `VariantRow`, `AttemptView`, `RowDetail`, `Phase`,
  `Section`, `Locale`, `Params`, `LoginField`, `Me`; `ApprovalState` and
  `ChannelStatus` are also used by tests). Dropping `export` would be cosmetic
  only.

  ### Checked and found sound
  - **`safeRedirect` / `decide`:** they block `//` and `/\`. Any remaining odd
  path (e.g. one containing a tab) still goes through `history.replaceState`
  with the same origin as the base, so there is no open redirect.
  - **No `v-html` anywhere:** AI and customer text renders as text only.
  - **Leaks:**
    - The EventSource is closed on unmount, on pause and on `end`.
    - The fetch stream is aborted on unmount and on leave.
    - All timers are cleared on unmount: ReviewView's interval, and the search,
  typing-pause and recipient timers.
    - The `beforeunload` listener is removed.
  - **Out-of-order fetches:** `RecipientsDrawer` and `CampaignsView` both use
  `seq` guards, and the drawer's guard also stops a request from filling a
  drawer that was just closed.
  - **Save queue (`createSaver`, D10):** saves run one at a time and the content
  is read when each save runs, so queued edits aren't lost. A 409 locks the
  page with a reload banner.
  - **Session-expiry loops:** the 401 paths in ContentView's leave guard and
  `commit` don't loop. Sign-out navigates first so the leave guard can still
  save.
  - **Double submits:** NewCampaignView, ReviewView `approve`, ResendDialog
  `create`, LoginView, the RuleEditor `saving` flag and the confirm dialogs
  (state cleared before acting) are all guarded.
  - **Locale switching:** `setLocale` picks the last request when two come
  quickly, survives a failed import and blocked storage, and the Turkish
  dictionary is typed so a missing key fails to compile.
  - **i18n keys:** keys built from template strings are type-checked against
  `Key`, and unknown codes go through `isKey`. I found no missing keys.
  - **ContentView auto-write (D71):** the `write` flag leaves the URL before the
  AI starts and the `gone` flag stops a write after leaving, so the AI starts
  at most once.
  - **Route guards:** pages are keyed by path, so each campaign gets its own
  instance and save queue, and `onBeforeRouteUpdate` only fires the leave guard
  when the campaign id changes.
  - **Recipients drawer:** closing returns to the earlier page via
  `history.state.back` when the drawer opened it, and replaces the URL
  otherwise.
  - **Pure helpers:** `briefTitle`, `smsInfo`, the SSE parser (CRLF and
  characters split across chunks), the schedule bounds and the approval blockers
  all match their decisions.

  I didn't run `docker compose … web-test` (tests and typecheck not executed)
  and didn't reproduce anything in a browser. Findings 2 and 4 depend on network
  timing and were traced in code only.

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Review: cmd/scc, campaign, auth, audience, content, config, apidocs, obs,
  spa

  I read every non-test .go file in scope in full, plus migrations 00001 and
  00006 for the constraints. I edited no files and ran no tests; every finding
  comes from tracing the code.

  ### Finding 1 (medium): `approve` and `approveLanguage` can deadlock the
  connection pool and freeze the whole API

  **Where**
  - `internal/campaign/approve.go:121-183`: `approve` starts a transaction with
  `h.Pool.Begin`, locks the row with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, then calls
  `h.loadContent` (lines 146 and 157) and `h.estimateFor` (line 183). Both take
  a second connection from the pool: `loadContent` via `h.Pool.Query`
  (content.go:449), `estimateFor` via
  `countAudience`/`audience.Count`/`content.EstimateOverLimit`, all on
  `*pgxpool.Pool`.
  - `internal/campaign/review.go:32-68`: `approveLanguage` does the same, with
  `loadContent` at lines 51 and 68 while its transaction is open.

  **Failure scenario**
  - pgx v5.11.0 sets the default pool size to max(4, NumCPU). I checked
  `pgxpool/pool.go:19` and `:384` in the module cache.
  - `DATABASE_URL` in docker-compose sets no `pool_max_conns`, so the default
  applies.
  - Say N = MaxConns marketers approve or approve-a-language at the same time,
  on any campaigns. Each request holds one connection for its transaction and
  then blocks in `Acquire` waiting for a second one. None can finish, so none
  frees a connection.
  - The waits use the request context, and the server has no WriteTimeout, so
  they only end when clients disconnect.
  - Meanwhile every other API request blocks too, because `auth.Require` needs a
  connection for its session lookup. The API hangs, not just the approvals.
  - With 4 connections that is 4 simultaneous clicks. It is unlikely, but the
  result is a total outage, not a 409.

  **How I verified it**
  - `grep "h.Pool.\|h.loadContent\|h.estimateFor"` in approve.go and review.go
  shows the pool calls listed above.
  - The other handlers that use transactions (`updateChannels`, `updateABTest`,
  `deleteDraft`, `cancel`, `saveContent`, `snapshot`, `listRecipients`) run only
  on the transaction until they commit, so they don't have this problem.

  **Smallest fix**
  - Run the reads inside these two handlers on the transaction itself. Change
  `loadContent`, `countAudience`/`estimateFor`, `audience.Count` and
  `content.EstimateOverLimit` to take a small interface with `Query`/`QueryRow`,
  which both `*pgxpool.Pool` and `pgx.Tx` satisfy, and pass `tx` from `approve`
  and `approveLanguage`.
  - A side benefit: approval would validate the content inside the same locked
  snapshot.
  - Raising `pool_max_conns` only raises the threshold; it does not remove the
  deadlock.

  ### Checked and sound (or covered by a decision)

  **Authorization and sessions**
  - Any marketer can read and change any campaign. D10 says so explicitly ("sees
  all campaigns"; "data isolation" means customer data), so this is not a
  finding.
  - Sessions: a random token whose SHA-256 is stored, a fixed 12 h expiry,
  HttpOnly with SameSite=Lax, the Secure flag when the request came over HTTPS,
  and logout deletes the session server-side.
  - Cross-site writes: the Origin check refuses non-GET requests from other
  origins, `Origin: null` included. All GET routes are read-only.
  - Login: the unknown-email path compares against a dummy bcrypt hash so it
  takes normal time. The throttle runs before the password check, is
  mutex-guarded, and sweeps old keys so the map can't grow without end.
  Resetting on success is part of D55.

  **SQL and input safety**
  - No SQL injection: audience rules, the search terms (escaped for LIKE), the
  template rendering inside SQL, and the recipient filters all reach the
  database as parameters. The only text built into SQL comes from fixed
  allowlists or integers.
  - `canonical()` can't skip the catalog check: pgx `CollectRows` returns an
  empty non-nil slice (rows.go:485), so an empty catalog still rejects names.
  - Rule validation has bounds, rejects repeated keys and unknown fields, and
  checks date windows. The AI's output is validated strictly, including the
  send-time offset and the length of the reason and note.
  - Content templates: allowlisted variables, control characters rejected, size
  caps on untrusted text, a single-line subject. The SQL rendering matches the
  Go rendering and has a test for it.

  **Optimistic locking and concurrency**
  - Version checks: every draft write is guarded with `WHERE version = $n AND
  status = 'draft'` or done under `FOR UPDATE`. `saveContent` re-checks the
  version after the long AI stream. `deleteDraft`, `approve`, `approveLanguage`
  and `cancel` all serialize on the row lock.
  - Resend duplicates are stopped by the `campaigns_one_active_resend_idx`
  unique index (23505 is mapped to 409). Active resend lookups return at most
  one row because of that index.
  - Content review rules match D66–D68: approval is reset when text changes,
  when a removed channel comes back, when the A/B test is turned on, and when
  the brief changes. Text of channels that aren't chosen is kept.
  - `snapshotCache`: the cached snapshot's time is only read and written under
  the mutex. The snapshot itself is published by closing the ready channel,
  which makes it safely visible to other streams. Errors are not cached, the
  read is not tied to one stream and has a 10 s timeout, and old entries are
  dropped.
  - The AI call limit is mutex-guarded and trims its list per marketer. The
  streaming check happens before the stream starts, and every provider call,
  retries included, counts (D63).

  **SSE**
  - The status stream ends on client disconnect, on server shutdown
  (`Stopping`), on a database error, or on the end state, and the ticker is
  stopped.
  - Content generation saves nothing once the client has gone. The LLM calls its
  text callback synchronously, so there are no concurrent writes to the
  response.

  **Resource handling**
  - All rows are closed (`defer rows.Close`, `CollectRows`, `ForEachRow`), every
  transaction has a deferred rollback, and every AI call's timeout context is
  cancelled.
  - Lists and pages are bounded: list page ≤ 10000 × 25 and recipients page ≤
  1000 × 50. Attempts for a page are loaded in one query (`ANY($1)`), so there
  is no N+1.

  **Static serving, metrics, config, startup**
  - spa: `path.Clean` stops path traversal. Precompressed files keep their
  original Content-Type, so they still work with nosniff. The CSP and the
  `/assets/` 404 behave as D56/D60 describe.
  - apidocs: its looser CSP applies to `/docs` only, the inline script is
  allowed by its hash, and the CDN files have SRI hashes (D73).
  - obs: metrics use the route pattern as the label. `Require` copies the
  pattern from its inner request back to the outer one, so guarded routes are
  labelled correctly. The status wrapper passes `Flush` and `Unwrap` through, so
  streams still flush.
  - config: every env var is validated with bounds. The provider's database
  password is restricted to URL-safe characters.
  - main: shutdown is graceful, and the worker stops if its HTTP server fails.

  **Dead code**
  - None. I grepped every exported identifier in scope across the whole repo,
  tests included. The only single hits were the JSON fields `Length` and `Part`
  and `statusWriter.Unwrap`, which `http.ResponseController` calls through the
  interface.

  I also looked at a few other items and dropped them: a 500 instead of a 404
  when a draft is deleted between two lookups, a delivery preview that is
  slightly off for languages whose only text is on a channel that isn't chosen,
  and no ReadTimeout (a deliberate choice for streaming, commented in main.go).
  None of them had a concrete failure worth reporting.

  **Not checked:** I did not reproduce the pool deadlock in Docker (that would
  mean writing a test with `pool_max_conns=1`), and I did not review the worker,
  the llm package or the provider package. They are out of scope, except that I
  confirmed the LLM callback is synchronous.

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Review: worker / provider / llm / db / dbtest / cmd/scc

  I read every non-test file in scope in full, plus the DECISIONS entries D5,
  D6, D7, D11, D18 and D19 and the code these files interact with
  (`campaign/approve.go` cancel, `audience.BuildResend`, `config` validation). I
  didn't edit or run anything. Every finding below comes from tracing the code
  and grepping for usages.

  **Result:** no high or medium defects. The queue, lease, retry and completion
  logic held up under adversarial tracing. Two low findings follow.

  ### Findings

  **L1 (low): a definitive provider answer is recorded as "interrupted" when
  shutdown races the response.** `internal/worker/dispatch.go:315-319`
  - **Scenario:** the provider answers 500, 429 or 503. SIGTERM, or a lost
  sender lock, cancels `ctx` before the `switch` runs. Because `case ctx.Err()
  != nil` comes before the retry and failure cases, the attempt is rewritten to
  `outcome="interrupted"`. That has three effects:
    - `HTTPStatus` stays at 500, contradicting the outcome.
    - The attempt doesn't count toward `MAX_ATTEMPTS`.
    - `Complete` sets `delivery_uncertain = true` (`queue.go:214`), so the UI
  says "may have been delivered" even though the provider explicitly said it was
  not.
  - **Impact:** the record is wrong, but nothing is double-sent, because the
  retry uses the same idempotency key.
  - **How I verified it:** traced `call()`, which returns the HTTP status even
  when `ctx` is cancelled after the response arrives, then the switch order,
  then the `delivery_uncertain` expression in `Complete`.
  - **Smallest fix:** only treat it as interrupted when no answer arrived: `case
  ctx.Err() != nil && attempt.HTTPStatus == 0:`.

  **L2 (low): dead production code.** `internal/worker/windowcap.go:48-61`
  - `windowCap.wait` is never called in production. `Process` runs its own
  `reserve()` loop (`dispatch.go:289-305`) so it can re-check cancel and consent
  between waits. Grep finds `.wait(` only in `windowcap_test.go` and
  `cap_order_test.go`.
  - **Fix:** delete it and have the tests drive `reserve()`, or leave it and
  accept a test-only helper. The same applies to `llm.NewMock()` (`mock.go:27`):
  production builds `&llm.Mock{ChunkDelay: …}` in `main.go:94`, and only tests
  call `NewMock`. Trivial either way.

  ### Checked and sound
  - **Claim:** the `IN (… LIMIT n FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED)` subquery re-checks
  status after locking. Ignoring `Query`'s error is safe because `CollectRows`
  returns `rows.Err()`. The index in 00009 matches the sort order.
  - **Lease vs in-flight send:** the worst-case path is about 0.2 s of limiter
  wait, about 1 s of cap wait, a 5 s HTTP timeout and a few database round
  trips, all well under the 30 s lease. A late `Complete` is fenced by
  `lease_token` plus `status='sending'`. A lost-lease attempt is still recorded,
  and `attempt_no` stays unique because every claim increments `attempt_count`.
  - **ReclaimExpired:** clears the token, so a stale `Complete` gets
  `ErrLeaseLost`. It marks the message uncertain, as D5 history intends.
  - **Retry and backoff:** `counted = prior non-interrupted attempts + 1`, and
  `>= MaxAttempts` means failed, so there's no off-by-one. `Backoff` can't
  panic, because config bounds are 100ms to 30s and 1 to 10 attempts.
  `Retry-After` is capped at 5 minutes. Retries take a limiter token and a cap
  slot.
  - **Cancel races:** cancel locks the campaign row `FOR UPDATE` and `buildOne`
  uses `SKIP LOCKED`. A claimed message re-checks campaign status after the
  limiter wait. `Complete` turns a retry into `cancelled` for a cancelled
  campaign. In the narrow case where `Complete` commits before cancel does, the
  message stays `retry_scheduled` briefly, then self-heals to `cancelled` at its
  next claim without sending.
  - **Campaign completion:** messages are created in the same transaction that
  sets `sending`. No code path moves a final message back to non-final (I
  grepped every `UPDATE messages`), so `FinishCampaigns` can't complete a
  campaign early.
  - **BuildAudience:** the `ON CONFLICT` dedup is correct. The A/B split is
  deterministic per language and the same across channels. Time zones work in
  the alpine image because `main.go` imports `time/tzdata`. Rules are re-parsed
  deterministically, so I found no persistent per-campaign error that could
  block later campaigns.
  - **Sender lock:** it uses its own connection with keepalives and a 1 s ping.
  On loss, sending stops and `wg.Wait` drains in-flight work. Results are
  written with `WithoutCancel`. The connection is closed only after the ping
  goroutine exits, so there's no goroutine or connection leak.
  - **Rate limiting:** `w.Rand` is guarded by a mutex, and `windowCap` is
  mutex-guarded with bounded memory. The `caps` and `Limiters` maps are
  read-only after `defaults()`.
  - **HTTP client:** has a client timeout. The response body is read up to a
  limit and then closed. The idempotency key is the message ID, the same on
  every attempt.
  - **Mock provider:**
    - The key is inserted before the outcome is simulated.
    - A key in progress gets a 503 with `Retry-After`.
    - A key released between the insert and the select is handled.
    - Completed keys replay the stored bytes.
    - The request body is size-capped and unknown fields are rejected.
    - `WithoutCancel` makes it finish work after the caller hangs up, which is
  intended.
  - **LLM:**
    - SDK retries are off (D15).
    - Callers apply timeouts (`interpret.go:87`, `content.go:146`).
    - Refusal and max-token stops are handled.
    - `decodeRuleValues` rejects trailing data and can't panic on a `null` rule.
    - The output is validated downstream in `campaign`.
    - Logs carry only timing and token counts, never the prompt, answer or key.
  - **FieldStreamer:** it is display only, it handles UTF-8 and surrogates split
  across chunks, and none of its stack indexing can panic.
  - **Mock LLM:** a regex must match the whole first sentence, anything it
  doesn't recognise is "unsupported", and its rules go through
  `audience.Validate`.
  - **db:** goose runs under a session advisory lock. The seed is all in one
  transaction and idempotent through `seed_meta`, and the sequences are moved
  past the seeded IDs. `SetProviderPassword` quotes with `%I` and `%L`, so it
  isn't injectable. The constraints in the migrations match the code's states
  and outcomes.
  - **cmd/scc:** the worker's HTTP server failure cancels the worker, shutdown
  is graceful with a 10 s limit, and no secrets are logged.
  - **Pool size:** the default pgxpool size is max(4, CPUs) for 41 worker
  goroutines. At about 7 round trips per message and 200 msg/s, that is under
  one connection busy on average, so it isn't a bottleneck.

  I didn't run the tests: every finding comes from tracing the code, and neither
  needs a test to show it. `dbtest` was reviewed only for leaks, since it is
  test-only. With the default timings, a mock "hang" followed by in-progress
  503s finishes within 5 attempts. With the allowed `MAX_ATTEMPTS=3` or
  `RETRY_BACKOFF_MAX=100ms`, such a message can end up `failed` but flagged
  `delivery_uncertain` while the provider still delivers it. I'm treating that
  as the D5/D7 design rather than a defect.

⏺ ESLint now runs in make lint and the CI lint job, and the review turned up
  five real bugs, all fixed. make test, make lint and make e2e all pass.
  Everything is on the chore/eslint-and-review branch, uncommitted, as you
  asked.

  ESLint

  - Rules: the recommended Vue and TypeScript rule sets, set up in
    web/eslint.config.js. Any warning fails the check. The six template-layout
    rules are off, as you chose.
  - CI: make lint now runs ESLint after gofmt and go vet, so the existing CI
    lint job covers it without workflow changes. make test runs the same checks
    as before.
  - Proof it bites: a planted any made make lint fail. A test .vue file with an
    untyped value and a list without keys got 4 errors.
  - Existing code: no lint errors, so no code changes were needed for lint.
  - Dependencies: 3 new dev dependencies at exact versions; none ship in the
    app.
  - Docs: D74 in DECISIONS.md, and the README lint mentions are updated.

  Review findings and fixes

  Three reviewers each read one area in full (API, worker/database, frontend). I
  checked every finding against the code myself, and each fix has a regression
  test that failed before the fix and passes now.

  #: 1
  Severity: Medium
  Issue: Approvals could freeze the whole API. Approving a campaign or a
  language
  held one database connection and then waited for a second. With the default
  pool of 4 connections, 4 approvals at the same moment would each hold one and
   wait forever, and every other request would hang too.
  Fix: The approval reads now run on the connection the approval already holds.
  New Go test TestApprove_NeedsOneConnection approves with a single-connection
  pool.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 2
  Severity: Medium
  Issue: An expired session on the delivery page never went to sign-in. The page

  checked the session with /api/me, whose 401 is deliberately ignored. The user
   got "Live updates stopped" with a Retry button that failed forever.
  Fix: It now checks with a request to the campaign itself, so a 401 sends the
  user to sign-in. New e2e check in delivery.mjs.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 3
  Severity: Medium
  Issue: Double-clicking "Write with AI" started two AI generations while other
  edits were still saving, and Stop could then only stop one of them.
  Fix: Added a guard so a second click is ignored. New e2e check in
  review-fixes.mjs; before the fix it counted 2 generations.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 4
  Severity: Low
  Issue: Checkboxes could show a state the page didn't have. After Cancel in the

  "Remove language?" dialog, the box stayed unticked. After a refused or failed
   A/B save, the switch stayed on.
  Fix: Both boxes are reset to the real state. New e2e check in
  languages-content.mjs.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 5
  Severity: Low
  Issue: Worker records were wrong during shutdown. A provider error that
  arrived
  as the worker was shutting down was recorded as "interrupted", not counted as
   an attempt, and marked "may have been delivered".
  Fix: "Interrupted" now applies only when there was no answer. New Go test
  TestProcess_AnswerDuringShutdownIsRecorded.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 6
  Severity: Low
  Issue: An older audience estimate could overwrite a newer one after two quick
  saves.
  Fix: Only the latest response is shown, using the same guard the campaign list

  and recipient drawer already use. No test, because it depends on network
  timing.

  Found but not fixed (judged not worth a change):
  - Malformed live-update messages: a broken message would throw an error on the
    page, but only our own server sends them, so it can't happen without a
    server bug.
  - Unused code: two small helpers are used only by tests (windowCap.wait in the
    worker and llm.NewMock). Removing them would mean rewriting tests for no
    behavior change.

  Checked and found sound: authorization, SQL injection, sessions and cross-site
  requests, the queue's leases, retries and cancel races, connection and timer
  cleanup, and protection against double submits elsewhere.

  Final results

  - make test: passes. All 14 Go packages pass, and the frontend passes 28 files
    / 437 tests plus the type check.
  - make lint: passes.
  - make e2e: passes. Every suite passed, 400 checks in total, including the 3
    new ones.
  - Secret scan (scripts/scan.sh tree): no leaks found.

  The A/B switch's failed-save path isn't covered by a test; only the
  cancelled-removal path is.

    text).

⏺ I found no confirmed business-logic bugs, so there are no code fixes. I added
  the "How it works" section to the README, and the changes are still
  uncommitted on chore/remove-dead-code. One test failed once in the first full
  run and passed on every rerun; details below.

  Workflow review: all six areas match DECISIONS.md and are covered by tests

  Area: Skipped vs failed
  What the code does: Each message is personalized right before every attempt.
  An
  SMS over 160 characters, or an email subject over 80, is marked skipped as
  too long and is never shortened or retried. Missing consent is skipped as no
  consent. Temporary provider errors (429, 5xx, timeouts, network errors) retry
   up to 5 attempts and then fail. Other 4xx responses fail at once.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Resend eligibility
  What the code does: Allowed once the campaign has completed or been cancelled,

  for one channel. It reaches only recipients skipped as too long, minus anyone
   already sent, still sending, with uncertain delivery, or who has since
  withdrawn consent. A unique index allows one non-cancelled resend per
  campaign and channel. I also traced resends of resends: they can't
  double-send, because any earlier resend must be cancelled first.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Shortening and resending
  What the code does: A resend is a new linked draft with its own content,
  estimate and approval. The AI is asked for shorter text. Its over-limit
  estimate is counted against the resend's own audience, and an empty audience
  can't be approved. The browser suite checks that the resend reaches exactly
  the eligible recipients and that none are too long again.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Consent
  What the code does: Checked when the audience is counted, when it is built,
  and
  again right before each attempt. A recipient who withdraws consent mid-send
  is skipped.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Personalization
  What the code does: The worker, the over-limit estimate and the preview all
  render text the same way. Values are flattened to one line, and an empty name
   leaves no stray space before punctuation. One theoretical gap: a customer
  value containing {{city}} would itself be filled in. It can't happen with
  today's seed data, and the estimate behaves the same way, so I didn't change
  it.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Duplicate prevention
  What the code does: One message per campaign, customer and channel, enforced
  by
  a unique constraint. The message ID is the provider's idempotency key, and
  the provider stores the key before sending. Retries, expired leases and
  double approvals can't produce a second message.

  README

  There was no step-by-step marketer walkthrough. I added a short How it works
  section between Setup and CASE requirements, with six steps: brief and
  audience, messages, review and approval, sending, monitoring, and resend. I
  checked every claim against the code and the decisions. While doing that I
  fixed two of my own draft mistakes: unsupported briefs are covered by D15 (not
  D14), and "other errors fail at once" now reads "other 4xx responses".

  Checks run

  - make test: passes on the final run. All 14 Go packages pass, and the
    frontend passes 437 tests.
  - make lint: passes.
  - Browser suites: delivery, recipients and resend all pass.
  - Secret scan: no leaks found.

  Intermittent test failure to decide on

  In the first full run, TestLifecycle_CancelMidCampaign_NoDepartureAfterCancel
  failed with "1 requests left after the cancel committed". It then passed 40
  times on its own, in 6 runs of the whole package, and in the final full run.

  - Cause: the worker checks the campaign status, records the send time, and
    then sends. If the cancel commits in the tiny gap after the check, that one
    request still goes out with a timestamp after the cancel.
  - Not from today's changes: this order dates from an earlier audit fix.
    Today's branch changes only README.md and the dead code, and PR #18's worker
    change runs after the provider call.
  - Fits the design: D11 says attempts already in progress may finish, so one
    in-flight send is allowed. The test asserts something slightly stricter,
    which makes it flaky under heavy CPU load.

  Closing this properly means one of two options, so it's your call:
  - Loosen the test (my recommendation): count only requests whose final status
    check started after the cancel, which is what D11 actually promises.
  - Lock the campaign row during the send: cancel would then wait up to the 5 s
    provider timeout.

  I changed neither.
